二、主要稅目別變動 
（一）8 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以證券交易稅增加 262 億元、營業
稅、營利事業所得稅及綜合所得稅分別增加 68 億元、66 億元及 61
億元較多。 
（二）累計 1 至 8 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 2,855
億元 、營利事業所得稅增加 1,599 億元及營業稅增加 906 億元較多。 
圖 2 主要稅目別變化

## Document 021: 2026-10-15_［みずほ経済インサイト］金利上昇による企業の負担増加はいつ本格化するか_eaa924d1

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-10-15_［みずほ経済インサイト］金利上昇による企業の負担増加はいつ本格化するか_eaa924d1.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

金利上昇による企業の負担増加は 
いつ本格化するか

○ 日銀の政策金利や、金融市場で決まる国債金利に比べ、企業が実際に負担する金利の上昇はまだ
限定的。企業の新規借入金利や負債残高全体への影響波及に時間がかかることが背景 
○ 金融機関の新規貸出金利は、概ね3カ月かけて政策金利の変化を反映する傾向。特に地域金融機関
で上昇がやや遅く、企業規模により有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが異なる一因に 
○ 借入・社債金利の動きを踏まえると、企業の利払い負担増加は2026～2028年度に本格化し、年間
約3兆円の利益下押し要因に。これまで以上に資本効率を意識した経営が求められる

１．政策金利や国債金利が上昇するも、企業の利払い負担増加はまだ本格化せず 
日本銀行は2026年9月の金融政策決定会合で、 政策金利を1.0％から1.25％へ引き上げた。これは1995
年以来、31年ぶりの高水準である1。また、金融市場で決まる10年国債金利（いわゆる長期金利）は、
2026年9月に節目となる3％を超えた。こちらも1996年以来30年ぶりの高さであり、政策金利や国債金
利はまさに歴史的な転換点を迎えている。 
一方で、 企業が実際に負担する金利の上昇は今のところ限定的だ。 図表1は、 企業の有利子負債利子
率 （＝支払利息等÷有利子負債残高） を指標金利 （政策金利、10年国債金利）と比較したものである。
図表 1 企業の有利子負債利子率と指標金利 図表2 企業の資金繰り判断 DI

（注） 有利子負債利子率は、 支払利息等を有利子負債 （短期 ・
長期借入金＋社債＋受取手形割引残高）の期首・期末
平均値で除した値、対象は金融・保険を除く全産業・
全規模 。凡例中の数値は、マイナス金利政策解除前
（2023年10～12月期）と2026年4～6月期の差 
（出所）日本銀行、財務省「国債金利情報」、「四半期別法人
企業統計調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成 
（注） （資金繰りが）「楽である」の回答率から「苦しい」
の回答率を差し引いた値。対象は全産業。大企業は資
本金10億円以上、中堅企業は1～10億円、中小企業は2
千万円～1億円未満

総合研究所調査部作成

図中の凡例にある数字は、 各金利についてマイナス金利政策解除 （2024年3月19日 ）前 の2023年10～12
月期と、直近の2026年4～6月期を比較した値だ。政策金利が＋0.93％Pt、10年国債金利が＋1.80％Pt
と大きく上昇した一方で、企業の有利子負債利子率は＋0.42％Ptと上昇幅が小さく、企業の利払い負
担の増加はまだ本格化していない。実際、日銀短観における企業の資金繰りに関する調査（図表2）を
みると、「楽である」の回答率から「苦しい」の回答率を差し引いたDIが、2021年以降プラス圏で概
ね横ばいを維持している。企業の実感としても、これまでに実施された複数回の利上げや、金融市場
における国債金利の大幅な上昇は、まだ資金調達環境の悪化につながっていないようだ。 
なぜ、こうしたギャップが生じるのか。主な要因として考えられるのは次の2点である。 
第一の要因は、指標金利と企業の新規調達金利とのタイムラグだ。政策金利などの指標金利が変化
してから、企業が新たに資金を調達する際の金利が変化するまで、少し時間がかかる。特に、企業の
有利子負債の半分強を占める金融機関からの借入において（図表3）、新規調達金利のタイムラグが生
じやすいと考えられる。 
第二の要因は、企業の新規調達金利と有利子負債残高全体の利払い負担とのタイムラグだ。企業が
新たに資金調達する際の金利が上昇しても、それが有利子負債残高全体にすぐ反映されるわけではな
い。 企業の金融機関借入金の4～5割程度が固定金利型であるほか2、 主に大企業が発行する社債の多く
が固定利付債であり、過去の低金利時代に調達した負債がまだ残っているためだ。 
本稿では、 これら2つの要因を踏まえて、政策金利・国債金利などの指標金利上昇を受けた企業の利
払い負担増加が、いつ本格的に現れるかを試算する3。 
２．金融機関からの新規借入金利は、地域金融機関で上昇ペースが遅い傾向 
まず、第一の要因（指標金利から企業の新規調達金利へのタイムラグ）の影響を検証しよう。図表
3に示したように、 企業の有利子負債には金融機関などからの借入金と、 主に大企業が発行する社債が
ある。このうち、社債を新規発行する際の金利は概ね国債金利に連動して決まり、タイムラグがほぼ
図表 3 企業の有利子負債残高の内訳 図表 4 金融機関業態別の新規貸出金利

（注） 2025年度の期初・期末平均。対象は金融・保険を除く
全産業・全規模。有利子負債には受取手形割引残高も
含まれるが、 シェアが小さい （0.1％程度） ため表示し
ていない。 
（出所）財務省「年次別法人企業統計調査」より、みずほ総合
研究所調査部作成 
（注） 当該期間中に金融機関が実行した円貸出の金利。企業
向け以外に、個人・政府・地公体向けの貸出も含む 
（出所）日本銀行「貸出約定平均金利の推移」より、みずほ総
合研究所調査部作成

### Page 3

3

無いため、ここでの検証は金融機関などから新規に借り入れる際の金利が対象になる。 
図表4は、金融機関の新規貸出金利（金融機関からみた貸出）を、業態別に示したものだ。都市銀行
では新規貸出金利の上昇ペースが速い一方、第二地方銀行や信用金庫では政策金利の上昇に比べて新
規貸出金利の上昇ペースがやや遅い。こうした地域金融機関における金利上昇の遅れが、指標金利と
企業の新規調達金利のタイムラグに影響していると考えられる。 
金融機関の業態によって新規貸出金利の上昇ペースに差があるのは、貸出の金利形態別シェアが異
なることが一因であろう。大手行では企業向け貸出全体の約6割を占める変動金利型貸出のうち、ほと
んどが「市場金利連動」であるため、金融市場の変化による影響がすぐに反映されやすい。他方で、
地方銀行や信用金庫では変動金利型貸出のうち「短期プライムレート連動」のシェアが高い。短期プ
ライムレートとは、各金融機関が設定する企業向け貸出の最優遇金利である。政策金利の変更などを
反映して決められることが多いが、各金融機関の競争環境などによって反映時期・度合いに差が生じ
る4。これにより、第二地方銀行や信用金庫では都市銀行に比べて新規貸出金利の上昇ペースが遅くな
る傾向があると考えられる5。 
こうした金融機関業態別の違いは、企業サイドにおいて、有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが規模別
に異なる一因になっている可能性がある。足元の金利上昇局面では、都市銀行からの借入が多いと考
えられる大企業（資本金10億円以上）で有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが速く、信用金庫など地域金
融機関からの借入が多いと考えられる小規模企業（同1千万円未満）で上昇ペースが遅い（図表5）。
2010年代の金利低下局面では大企業の有利子負債利子率が小規模企業を下回る傾向にあったが、足元
では完全に逆転している。大企業は社債が有利子負債残高の約3分の1を占めるため、金融機関の新規
貸出金利のみが原因とは言えないものの、金融機関業態別の違いが企業規模別の資金調達環境に影響
を及ぼしている可能性がある点は興味深い。 
では、今後日銀がさらに利上げを進めていくと、金融機関の新規貸出金利はどの程度のペースで、
どこまで上昇していくか。過去の政策金利と新規貸出金利の関係を統計的に分析すると、 政策金利1％

### Page 4

4

Ptの上昇は新規貸出金利を当月に0.31％Pt押し上げ、その後さらに3カ月間かけて当月からの累計で
0.85％Pt押し上げる傾向があることが明らかになった。日銀が今後2027 年半ばにかけて政策金利を
2.0％まで引き上げていくことを前提にすると6、金融機関の新規貸出金利（2026年8月時点で1.78％）
は2027年秋ごろにかけて2.65％程度へ上昇すると試算される （図表6） 。 マイナス金利政策の解除前と
比較すると、2％Pt弱の上昇になる計算だ。 
３．企業の利払い負担増加は今後 2028 年度にかけて本格化。 利益を年間約 3 兆円下押し 
次に、第二の要因（企業の新規調達金利と有利子負債残高全体の利払い負担とのタイムラグ）の影
響を検証しよう。金融機関の新規貸出金利や社債の新規発行金利の上昇が、それぞれの残高全体にど
の程度のペースで反映されるかを確認し、企業の利払い負担がいつ本格化に増加するか試算する。 
金融機関の新規貸出金利と貸出残高全体の金利を比較すると、今回の金利上昇局面では新規貸出金
の上昇に対して貸出金利残高全体の金利上昇がやや遅れている（図表7）。 先に述べたように、金融機
関の貸出の一部が固定金利型であり、新規貸出や借り換えに伴って新規貸出金利の上昇が残高全体へ
波及するのに時間がかかるためだ。 
両者の過去の関係を統計的に分析すると、新規貸出金利の上昇がおよそ5年程度かけて残高全体の金
利を緩やかに押し上げる傾向があるとの結果になった。 図表6で試算したように、 先行きの新規貸出金
利が2027年秋ごろにかけて2.65％程度まで上昇していくと想定すると、貸出残高全体の金利（2026年
8月時点で1.51％ ）は2027年末に2.0％程度、2028年末に2.3％程度と緩やかに上昇し、2030年代前半に
は新規貸出金利と同じ2.65％程度へ収束していく計算になる。 
同様に社債について、 新発債金利と既発債金利を比較したものが図表8だ。 新発債金利は2022年ごろ
から上昇して足元では2％台後半に達しているが、既発債金利はまだ1％強にとどまっており、金融機
関の貸出金利に比べると残高全体への反映に時間がかかりやすいことがうかがえる。なお、日本で新

### Page 5

5

規に発行される社債の平均年限は現在5～6年であり、新発債金利は概ね5 年国債金利に連動して動く
傾向があることから、先に述べたように指標金利→新発債金利のタイムラグはほとんど生じない。 
今後、社債残高が物価上昇にあわせて年間＋2％のペースで増加していくとの前提を置き、 先行きの
償還に伴う借り換えや追加的な新規発行が行われていく場合、既発債金利は2030年代初めにかけて年
間＋0.3％Pt程度の緩やかなペースで上昇していくと試算される（新発債金利は5年国債金利の先行き
予測値に連動すると想定）。7年債や10年債といった年限が長めの社債も一定の規模があることから、
新発債金利の上昇が既発債金利に反映されるまでに時間を要する結果になった。 
最後に、金融機関の貸出金利（企業からみた借入金利7）と社債金利の試算結果を用いて、企業の有
利子負債利回りへの影響を検証する。企業の有利子負債に占める借入と社債の金額シェアを先行き一
定として、 貸出金利と社債金利の変化を有利子負債利子率に反映したものが図表9だ。2023～2025年度
の有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースは年間＋0.1～＋0.2％Pt程度と緩やかだったが、2026～2028年度に
は同＋0.3～＋0.4％Ptに加速すると見込まれる。企業の有利子負債の多くを占める借入金の金利上昇
が、主な押し上げ要因だ。2029年以後は借入金の金利上昇が落ち着く一方、社債金利の上昇継続を受
け、有利子負債利子率は2030年代にかけて緩やかに3％程度の水準まで上昇していくだろう。 
このように、金利上昇による企業の利払い負担増加は今後2028年度にかけて本格化していく見込み
だ。企業全体の有利子負債残高が先行き年間＋2％のペースで拡大していくと仮定すると、 支払利息等
が年間約3兆円増加する計算であり、経常利益（金融・保険を除くベースで2025年度：124.8兆円）に
対し年間2.4％の押し下げ要因に相当する8。 
今後、有利子負債利子率が上昇していく過程では、それに見合った利益率を達成できるかが問われ
るだろう。2025年度時点では投下資本利益率（ROIC、3.70％）の有利子負債利子率（1.45％）に対す
るスプレッドが2％Ptを上回っており、ROICでみた企業の資本効率は外部資金調達コストを十分カバー
できている（図表10）9。しかし、 有利子負債利子率が今後3％程度へ上昇していくと、ROICが現在の水
準のままではスプレッドがコロナ禍初期の2020年度並みに縮小し、企業の利払い負担に対する利益の
図表 9 企業の有利子負債利子率の試算 図表 10 有利子負債利子率と投下資本利益率

残高と社債残高のシェアを一定として、有利子負債利

［参考文献］ 
みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）「2026年・2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ～転換点は近いのか、 投資
主導経済の行方を探る～」、2026年10月2日

1 現在の政策金利は無担保コール翌日物金利の誘導目標だが、1995 年時点では公定歩合を政策金利と見なして時系列比較してお
り、厳密には政策金利の意味するものが異なる。 
2 日本銀行「金融システムレポート」 （2026年4 月）の図表Ⅲ-1-12 によれば、法人向け貸出のうち、大手行で約40％、地域銀
行（地方銀行、第二地方銀行）や信用金庫で 45～50％程度が固定金利貸出である（2025 年 9 月末時点）。 
3 金利上昇による企業への影響には、利払いの増加によるデメリットだけでなく、利子収入の増加によるメリットもある。本稿
では、そのうちデメリットの面だけに焦点を当てて分析しており、メリットについては稿を改めて論じたい。 
4 日本銀行「金融システムレポート」 （2025年4 月）の図表Ⅵ-1-8 では、地域銀行を対象に、2024 年7 月の政策金利上昇を受け
た短期プライムレートの引き上げタイミング、引き上げ幅、引き上げ後金利の貸出先適用割合について分析が行われている。 
5 図表 4 に示した金融機関業態別の新規貸出金利には、企業向け以外の貸出も含まれる。したがって、企業向け貸出における
「市場金利連動」 、 「短期プライムレート連動」のシェアが業態別に異なることだけでなく、貸出全体に占める住宅ローン貸出
のシェアが業態別に異なることなども、新規貸出金利の上昇ペースの違いに影響していると考えられる。 
6 みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）の見通しに基づく想定。 
7 金融機関の貸出金利には企業向け以外の貸出も含まれるが、ここでは簡易的に、企業向け貸出金利が貸出全体の金利に連動す
ると仮定して試算した。 
8 本稿では金利上昇による利払いの増加のみを計算対象としているため、利子収入の増加も考慮すれば経常利益への下押し圧力
は緩和すると考えられる。 
9 投下資本利益率（ROIC）と本来比較すべきは、負債だけでなく自己資本のコストも考慮した加重平均資本コスト（WACC）だ
が、本稿では金利上昇の影響を直接評価する観点から、投下資本利益率と有利子負債利子率を比べている。

市場焦點
美國
• 9月ISM服務業PMI降至54.9，雖商業活動與新訂單放緩 ，但就業回升至 50.1、訂單積壓增加 ，
顯示服務需求仍具韌性；另一方面，價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，供應商交貨
速度亦放緩 ，反映燃料 、原物料及供應鏈成本升高 ，景氣未明顯轉弱之際 ，通膨壓力反而重
新升溫；同時全球長債殖利率同步走高 ，AI基建投資延續企業成長動能 ，也提高資本需求 。
整體而言 ，美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但通膨與長端利率壓力同步升高 ，使金融條件維持偏緊 。
中國
• 彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與 AI普及下，中國產業快速發展出現供給過剩 ，產業獲利
能力面臨挑戰，未來三年中國AI產業將可能處於虧損狀態，不過即使模型價格下降，AI使用量
增加將帶動Token需求大幅成長，其中具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢的AI龍頭，將比單

純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
黃金
• 歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵 ，加上長期通膨風險升溫 ，宏觀不確定性讓實體黃金
市場逆勢吸金 。本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易 ，實體黃金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中
期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以投資組
合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

美國9月ISM服務需求略降、成本壓力升高，景氣韌性與通膨風險並存
• 服務業維持擴張，需求略降但就業回穩：9月ISM服務業指數降至54.9(前月55.4)，連續27個月位於擴張區間；商業活動
指數降至56.5、新訂單降至 59.8，顯示服務需求較前月放緩 ，但就業指數回升至 50.1、重返擴張區間 ，訂單積壓升至
56.6，顯示內需仍具韌性，服務業尚未出現明顯轉弱訊號。
• 成本與供應鏈壓力升高 ，通膨降溫面臨干擾： 價格指數升至 74.0，創2022年7月以來新高 ，且為7個月來第6次高於70；
供應商交貨指數升至53.2，顯示交貨速度放緩，燃料、柴油、鋼材、銅及記憶體等成本上升，加上供應受限，使服務業
投入成本壓力擴大，通膨降溫進程面臨干擾。
• 市場觀察：美國服務業為經濟成長的重要支撐，目前經濟呈現「需求仍強、通膨升溫」的拉鋸格局，雖服務業景氣支撐

美國企業獲利，但成本上升可能推升利率波動，投資上宜偏向美國短中天期投資級債，兼顧較高收益率與利率風險控管；
股票則可聚焦具定價能力、獲利與現金流穩健的優質企業。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6 資料來源：ISM Services PMI Report， 2026.10.6

全球利率同步走高、AI投資持續擴張，長端美債壓力仍高
• 全球長債殖利率同步走高，反映利率環境重新定價：美國財政部長貝森特指出，近期美債殖利率攀升與德國、日本等主
要債市同步上行，反映全球資本成本重新定價，而非市場對美國資產失去信心；加上目前尚未出現資金大規模撤離美債
並轉向其他主權債券的現象，因此債市波動較偏向全球利率環境調整，而非金融系統性風險。
• AI投資增加資金需求，支撐較高利率環境：貝森特亦駁斥AI泡沫論，認為微軟、Alphabet及Meta等大型科技企業仍以
實際獲利與現金流支撐持續擴大AI資本支出，不同於過去缺乏基本面的科技泡沫；龐大的資料中心 、電力與半導體投資
需求提高整體資金成本，也成為推升長天期利率的重要因素之一。
• 市場觀察：目前市場經濟近況屬於利率高檔、成長尚穩的情境中，因而投資策略建議配置大型科技股、AI基建供應鏈及

受惠利差環境的金融股具相對優勢；債券配置則以中短天期投資級債優先，避免長天期債券受利率上行壓力侵蝕。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6

• 美國8月貿易逆差擴大至2025年初以來最高水平：美國提高進口關稅後，貿易失衡問題仍未明顯改善，商務部數據顯示，8

師預期第三季標普500企業獲利年增約29.5%，加上企業獲利預期持續上修，強化投資人對高利率環境下企業基本面的信心。
投資策略上，相較於單筆投資，此時的高成長與高波動的環境，更適合分批布局的定期定額策略來參與市場。

獲利成長；中長期建議維持日股正向看法，聚焦金融、AI、機器人、自動化及電力設備等具結構性成長題材的企業。

中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境
• 中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境 ：彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與
AI普及下，中國AI產業快速發展 ，但也逐漸出現供給過剩 ，使產業獲
利能力面臨挑戰 。開源模式雖有助於降低技術門檻 、應用普及 ，但也
使技術優勢難以長期維持 ，加上中國市場長期習慣低價甚至免費的數
位服務，多數AI業者難以透過訂閱模式獲利，轉而依賴利潤較低的API
服務作為主要收入來源 。目前中國已備案大型語言模型 (LLM)數量達
988個，包括DeepSeek、Kimi及智譜AI等新創企業快速崛起，進一步
加劇市場競爭。此外API服務逐漸被視為基礎公用服務，即使AI Agent
帶動詞元(Token)需求快速成長，也未必能同步提升獲利，由於開源模
型可由不同雲端業者部署 ，市場進入門檻降低 ，業者更難建立長期競

爭優勢。
• 後市展望： 考量龐大資本支出與持續價格競爭 ，未來三年中國 AI產業
整體仍可能處於虧損狀態 ，市場將形成「美國閉源、高毛利」與「中
國開源 、低成本 」模式 ，即使模型價格下降 ，AI 使用量增加將帶動
Token需求大幅成長 ，進而推升資料中心 、雲端服務及 AI晶片需求 ，
從投資角度來看，基礎設施供應商(晶片、伺服器、資料中心、雲端)受
惠程度可能高於模型開發商；而具有企業客戶 、專有資料及生態優勢
的AI龍頭，將比單純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。

人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟
和市場信心
• 人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台 ，有望提振中國經濟和市場信心 ：日前中國推出購房貸款貼息政策及下調
PSL(抵押補充貸款)利率，代表穩增長政策進一步加碼 ，有助於提振市場信心與經濟預期 ，此次政策不僅支持房地產市場 ，也
將「六張網」建設、科技創新、民營及小微企業 ，納入重點扶持範圍 ，透過增加再貸款額度 ，與提高支持比例 ，帶動投資、
就業及消費回升。短期A股有望在政策推動下出現築底反彈，其中基建投資、製造業投資、房市銷售及居民消費有機會改善，
同時PSL(抵押補充貸款)降息有助於流動性環境邊際寬鬆 ，降低市場對經濟增長的疑慮 ，後市來看企業獲利與信用環境有望逐
步修復，進一步鞏固A股慢牛格局。投資建議上房貸貼息政策有利房地產、建材、家居等產業鏈，金融業受惠資金成本下降與

信貸回升，食品飲料、零售、家電及服務消費則可望受惠於居民消費意願提升。

中國、日本減持美國公債的背後邏輯與差異
• 橋水基金創始人達利歐警告 ，美國公債正面臨中日兩國減持的風險 ，數據顯示這項警告已成事實 。2026年前七個月，中國
美國公債持倉減少660億美元至6,180億美元；同期日本持倉亦減少820億美元至1.1兆美元，扣除價格變動的估值效應後，
兩國實質持倉均大幅下滑。然而，兩國削減美國公債的背後動機並不相同，這將導致未來的減持軌跡出現明顯分化。
• 中國戰略性長期去美元化：1.降低地緣政治風險與轉向資產多元化 ，隨著人民幣在國際貿易結算中使用增加 ，市場流動性提
升，中國對美元儲備依賴度顯著下降。2.美債財政可持續性隱憂，美國政府債務持續攀升，引發對美國長期財政健康疑慮。
3.根據彭博經濟研究模型預測，在漸進式減持情境下，中國的美元儲備(以美債與機構債為主)到2050年可能減少約50%。

• 中國搭配戰術性節奏調整 (短期緩和因素)：儘管長期趨勢向下 ，但目前中美利差顯著 (美中30年期公債利差超過 350個基點)，
在美債殖利率維持高位期間，過快減持意味著實現債券價格下跌的虧損，且可能加劇人民幣升值壓力。因此，中國短中期內
傾向採取「戰略性減持、戰術性放緩」的靈活步調。
美國公債持倉：中國 vs 日本
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，美國財政部
中國預計將減少美元儲備
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，中國央行、國際貨幣基金組織、彭博經濟研究。

日本受政策利率與本土資本回流驅動
• 與中國的結構性考量不同，日本的減持主要出自週期性因素與匯率干預需求。
• 日本央行貨幣政策轉鷹： 日本央行正加快升息腳步 。隨著日債殖利率上揚 (30年期日債殖利率升至約 4.2%)，美日利差持續
收窄，吸引日本國內機構資金回流本土主權債市場。
• 匯率干預政策：日本為防範日圓過度貶值，日本財務省傾向出售部分海外美債資產以籌集外匯干預資金。若日圓再次面臨壓
力，此類拋售干預可能持續。因此，隨著日本央行逐步走向政策正常化，日本對美債的持倉大概會延續下滑軌跡。
• 小結與投資建議：美國公債作為全球無風險資產的地位正面臨結構性挑戰，中國的「長期去美元化」與日本的「本土利率回
升資金回流」將形成雙重壓力，這意味著美債殖利率短期內難大幅下行，美國財政部發債成本居高不下，進而推高全球資本

的融資成本。投資債券配置建議採取「中短天期＋階梯式配置」策略，長天期美國公債(20年以上)受發債供給過剩及中日兩
國長期減持影響，價格波動較大。建議投資人適度降低長天期美國公債暴險，轉向防禦性較佳的中短天期美國公債或高品質
高評級公司債，以鎖定當前較高殖利率同時控管利率風險。
殖利率差：美元與人民幣 vs 美元與日圓
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.29

黃金市場資金流向分歧，實體投資有望支撐中長期金價表現
• 利率與匯率因素，壓抑金價近期表現：9月以來，美國公債殖利率飆升、美元指數走揚，由於黃金本身不孳息，資金傾向轉
往固定收益市場，疊加美元走強壓抑國際購買意願，金價自8月底高點至今已下修約11%。
• 9月資金流向分歧，實體投資強、期貨市場弱：根據世界黃金協會統計，9月在金價下跌之際，全球實體黃金ETF卻吸引大量
資金流入，流入約100億美元(67噸)。另一方面，COMEX黃金期貨市場投機部位大幅下降 ，避險基金與其他機構投資人的
多頭部位明顯減少，造成期貨市場賣壓增加，進一步推動金價下跌。
• 實體黃金市場逆勢吸金反應投資人的避險需求：歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上美伊衝突未解及AI算力需求強
勁，長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀的不確定性讓實體黃金市場逆勢吸金。從數據上來看，本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃

金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中期的支撐 。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以
投資組合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026.10.7。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。

非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。

歐美航線需求放緩 ，預估 4Q26
稅後 EPS 14.89 元 
 根據上海航交所 10/9 公告，上海出口集裝箱運價指數 (SCFI) 3,664.11 點
(+0.05%WoW，+215.76%Y oY)，隨中國十一長假結束，歐線運價短期反彈，
分別為遠東-歐洲線+3.41%WoW，遠東-地中海線+0.2%WoW；美線持平，遠
東-美西線+0.08%WoW，遠東-美東-0.04%WoW。觀察 10/11 公告跨大西洋、
歐 洲 及 跨 太 平 洋 航 線 運 力 分 別 變 動 -1.14%WoW 、 -1.88%WoW 及
+0.48%WoW。整體而言，隨歐美航線逐步進入傳統淡季，航商陸續調整運力
配置，其中歐洲線運力投放減少，美線則大致持平，顯示航商持續透過運力調
節支撐市場運價。

展望 4Q26，需求面而言，歐線隨傳統旺季結束，終端需求動能轉弱，預期後
續運價將呈緩步回落走勢。美線方面，旺季需求已近尾聲，且部分船班裝載率
已有下滑，儘管 Sep-26 川習會後美國調降部分消費品關稅，包括玩具、小型
家電及節慶裝飾品等，惟目前尚未出現明顯大型備貨潮。供給面而言，隨水情
陸續改善 ， 巴拿馬運河管理局預計自2026/10/15 起將每日允許通行量由 32 艘
回升至 33 艘，惟仍低於原先 36 艘水準。考量大型貨櫃船通行具一定優先性，
目前對船期營運影響相對有限，惟通行量尚未完全恢復，仍可能使美東線有效
運力有所收斂。另，美伊衝突及紅海通行風險仍存， Gemini 聯盟持續恢復部
分紅海航線，近期亦傳出 Premier Alliance 有望於 4Q26 恢復部分紅海航線，
若後續其他航商陸續跟進，繞航所吸收之有效運力恐逐步釋放，對市場運價形
成壓力。

整體而言，儘管近洋線進入傳統旺季，惟長榮歐美航線營收占比較高，預期整
體運價及貨量將隨淡季到來而回落 ， 預估4Q26 營收 1,350.87 億元(-8%QoQ，
+57.6%Y oY)。獲利方面，儘管油價仍維持高位，惟隨貨量減少，相關變動成
本亦有望下降，預估毛利率 37.9%。另考量長榮往年於 4Q 提列年終獎金費
用，預期費用率將季增，預估營益率 29.4%。假設匯率持穩，預估稅後淨利
323.53 億元(-23.8%QoQ，+279.8%Y oY)，4Q26 稅後 EPS 14.94 元。

2026 年有效運力供給受限 ， 預估
稅後 EPS 45.75 元 
 展望後市，根據 Clarksons Aug-26 預測，2026 年全球貨櫃供給年增 4.6%，需
求年增 3.8%，供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年逐步收斂。短期而
言，受到缺工及天候因素影響，部分港口作業效率下降，近期塞港現象增加，
有助吸收部分有效運力。展望 2027 年，在尚未考慮紅海全面復航之前提下，
Clarksons 預估全球貨櫃供給+8.8%Y oY，需求+3.5%YoY，供需缺口將再度擴
大；若紅海航線進一步恢復，繞航所吸收之有效運力亦將逐步釋放，供給壓力
恐進一步增加。需求方面，則須持續關注停戰協議進展、美國關稅政策變化及
通膨壓力對終端需求之影響。

展望 2026 年，長榮持續優化航線布局，強化東南亞、中東、非洲及中南美洲
等區域配置 ， 並透過長約布局降低市場運價波動影響，整體而言 ，元富預估 2026
年營收 4,735.4 億元(+24.9%Y oY)。獲利方面，預估毛利率 30.8%，營益率
25.4%，稅後淨利 991.6 億元(+44.6%Y oY)，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 45.8 元。
展望 2027 年，隨新船陸續交付，全球貨櫃船隊運力持續增加，儘管塞港情況
仍可能限縮有效運力，惟考量整體供給成長仍高於需求，預期市場運價將逐步
回歸常態。整體而言，預估 2027 年營收 4,097.3 億元(-13.5%Y oY)，稅後淨利
482.4 億元(-51.4%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 22.28 元。

### Page 1

ASE Technology Holding

3Q26 sales outperformance affirms solid 
transformation progress

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 900.00 TWD | Price: 762.00 TWD

3Q26 sales beat on LEAP ramp and mature ASP strength 
ASE reported 3Q26 sales +28% QoQ better than its guide, driven by stronger IC ATM 
(+20% QoQ vs. BofAe +13% QoQ) and mild upside in EMS (+42% QoQ vs. BofAe +40% 
QoQ). We believe IC ATM strength is led by modest mainstream packaging and testing 
price hike on industry supply tightness, supplemented by LEAP business ramp. EMS also 
benefited from Apple product seasonal builds and higher component cost pass through. 
With improving packaging and testing utilization and ASP and rising LEAP mix, we 
expect its IC ATM GMs to improve 300bps QoQ to 30% (vs. high-20% guide) though 
consolidated GMs (+180bps QoQ to 23%) could be partially offset by EMS outgrowth. 
AI pipeline diversification anchors capex plan in ’26-28 
We model ASE’s 4Q26 IC ATM sales +7-9% QoQ as its LEAP opportunity diversifying 
from TSMC outsourcing to direct project wins including AMD’s Venice CPU, putting sales 
on track to +42% YoY in ’26 (vs. its +35% guide). Its IC ATM GMs could further expand 
to 31% as LEAP reaching 30% of its sales (vs. 18%/22% in 1H/3Q26). While we expect 
ASE to spend US$13bn+ annual capex in ’27-28 (vs. US$10.5bn in ’26), the accelerating 
IC ATM sales (BofAe +40% CAGR from ’26-28) on advanced expansion and mature price 
uplift, along with the business mix upgrade to cloud AI/HPC projects carrying 35-45% 
GMs should offset hiking depreciation (BofAe +39% CAGR from ’26-28), lifting its IC 
ATM GM from 29% in ’26 to low 30% in ’27 and mid 30% in ’28 with operating leverage. 
Stronger earnings quality unlocks re-rating potential 
We lift our ’26/27/28E EPS to NT$20/NT$34/NT$50 on strong LEAP outlook. Keep Buy 
rating and raise POs from NT$800 (US$51) to NT$900 (US$58) still on 20x 2H27-1H28 
P/E (vs. its 5-26x range) backed by its structural business transformation with better 
sales and GMs growth profile as the industry supply further consolidates amid higher 
tech entry barrier.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Established in 1984 and headquartered in Taiwan, ASE 
Technology Holding is the no.1 outsourced assembly and 
testing (OSAT) firm globally, in terms of market share by 
revenue. ASE acquired Siliconware Precision (SPIL) in 2018, 
and had acquired USI in 2010, which became its EMS 
business segment providing synergies in system-level 
packaging. 2 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
We rate ASE Buy, factoring in the company's opportunity in 
advanced packaging and testing evidenced in TSMC's 
outsourcing and its own heavy capex investment. The 
strategy shift should allow the company to capture fast 
growing addressable market in high performance 
computing applications and improves its margins. ASE 
could maintain its leading position in the Outsourced 
Semiconductor Assembly and Test (OSAT) universe, owing 
to its well-established footprints and widespread service 
offerings.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
ASE Technology Holding (XSRIF / ASX) 
We value ASE Technology Holding at NT$900 per share (US$58 for ADR), based on 20x 
2H27-1H28E P/E, above its historical range of 5-26x, in view of its solidified industry 
position in AI era. We use P/E ratio to value ASE as the company has been able to 
maintain healthy profitability through the cycles in the past 20 years due to 
consolidating industry landscape supporting a more benign supply/demand and keeping 
pricing and margins less cyclical.

Downside risks to our PO are 1) share loss and/or ASP erosion owing to Chinese players' 
cannibalization and/or ASE's worsening execution, 2) weakening end demand due to 
uncontrollable matters, such as macro halt and/or geopolitical tensions, 3) regulatory 
issues that could hinder ASE from generating synergies and benefit its competitors.

## Document 128: 261015_daiwa_TSMC

Source PDF: all report pdf/261015_daiwa_TSMC.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: TSMC continued delivering “beat-n-raise” prints for its 
quarterly results and guidance on 15 October. While we raise our forecasts 
to factor in its better topline and pricing trajectory yet muted margin 
expectation on an incremental basis, we see likely lack of near-term 
passion from investors on limited positive surprises from its results call this 
time. That said, we believe AI demand remains strong and sustainable to 
support our Buy (1) call on the stock, though patience may be necessary 
for investors amid the current jittery environment.

What’s the impact: Results intact, guidance mixed. Despite 3Q26 
topline of USD46.7bn topping guidance (USD44.6-45.8bn) at TSMC, its 
3Q26 EPS came in at TWD29.4, only marginally beating our/Bloomberg 
consensus estimates on lower opex and higher non-operating gains from 
long-term investments more than offsetting lower gross margin associated 
with higher depreciation costs on dilutive N2 ramp (table on p.2). Guidance 
calls for higher topline for 4Q26 with midpoint of the range 6% above our 
previous forecast, thanks to continued AI strength as customers continue to 
increase demand. Yet margins at the operating level looked muted without 
further operating leverage being expected by the street, due to the margin-
dilutive N2 ramp. We raise our 2026-28E forecasts by 3-10% to factor in 
the better topline trajectory, plus our view of better pricing power.

Lack of near-term passion? Apart from the upbeat topline but muted 
margin guidance on an incremental basis, we left the results call with 
limited positive surprises this time which may not drive any passion for 
investors in the near term to catalyse the stock. That said, fundamentals 
remain strong since AI demand continues to mushroom from accelerators 
to CPU and other companion chips, as language models transform from 
generative to agentic for AI assistance. TSMC implied upside risk to its 
long-term AI accelerator revenue CAGR of mid-to-high-50% over 2024-29 
and, as flagged in our Asian Foundry report, we saw upside risk to TSMC’s 
pricing power for 2027, given the still meaningful supply constraint where 
management reiterated its strong conviction in this multi-year AI megatrend 
and active capacity build aimed to narrow the supply/demand gap.

What we recommend: We raise our 12M TP for TSMC to TWD3,200 
(previous: TWD3,000), based on our finetuned ROE-adjusted PBR target of 
7.5x one-year forward BVPS forecast (previous: 7.7x). We reiterate our Buy 
(1) call with key downside risks of “silicon inflation” and geopolitical 
overhangs leading to global inflation and AI capex cut.

How we differ: Our 2028E EPS is 11% below the Bloomberg consensus 
likely on our view calling for a soft-landing in the chip cycle.

Company profile 
Incorporated in Taiwan in 1987, Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMC) is the world’s largest 
complementary metal oxide semiconductor (CMOS) foundry in revenue terms. TSMC offers foundry 
services such as wafer masking, fabrication, probing, packaging and testing, to a high variety of 
customers including fabless chipmakers and IDMs. Its manufacturing fabs are located in Taiwan, China, 
the US and Singapore.

### Page 5

5

Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (2330 TT): 15 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 15 Oct 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
TSMC's board of directors (BoD) has 10 members, including 6 independent directors
representing 60% of total directors. The background of TSMC's BoD is diversified, with members
from industries, academia, and law. Also, its board members include representatives from not
only Taiwan but also Europe and the US, bringing their experience in operating international
businesses to the board.
We believe the high percentage of independent directors and a diverse mix of expertise from the
board members strengthen the functionality of its BoD, as evidenced by TSMC's leading position
in the foundry market.
Capital management 1
TSMC pays its dividend on a quarterly basis. In the past 5 years, it has managed its dividend
payout well, as evidenced by its dividend in dollar terms, where it has never lowered its dividend.
As the leader in advanced technology nodes, TSMC has kept expanding its capacity for
advanced nodes; as such, we expect its capital intensity to increase to the level near or above
40% over 2025-26 (vs. its long-term target of mid-30%). However, we believe TSMC will at least
sustain its cash dividend payout in dollar terms over 2025-26E. We see its balance between
investment and dividend as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1
TSMC's sales to related parties and purchases from related parties remain immaterial as a
percentage of total revenue and cost of goods sold in 2026. We view these levels as insignificant
and therefore see limited risk to TSMC's earnings, cash flow generation, or corporate
governance.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
As the leader in the global foundry industry, TSMC approaches supply chain management by
focusing on 2 main strategies: sustainability risk management and local supply chain
optimisation. It requires all its suppliers to comply with its "Code with Ethics and Business
Conduct", and to follow regulations on human rights and conflict-free minerals.
In 2019, 100% of its tier-1 suppliers signed the "Supplier Code of Conduct" and self-assessment
questionnaire on sustainable management. Also, TSMC's raw materials purchased are 100%
Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) conflict-free. It also requires its critical suppliers to
report on the status of sustainability management in their critical upstream supply chains.
Through these measures, we see TSMC reducing its supply chain risk.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
TSMC applies internal and external company resources to develop water reclaimation
technology, implements water conservation measures and uses reeclaimed water in the
manufacturing process. For example, on September 19, 2022, TSMC Tainan Science Park
Reclaimed Water Plant started supplying reclaimed water. In 2025, TSMC aims to replace 5% of
water resources with reclaimed water, and continue to cooperate with the government to
complete the 2nd water reclamation plan in Anping, Tainan. In 2030, TSMC targets to replace >
60% of water resources with reclaimed water.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
TSMC's waste recycling rate reached 96% in 2022 for 8 years in a row. Also, the percentage of
waste sent to landfills was less than 1% for 13 years in a row in 2022.
TSMC achieved reducing its outsourced unit waste disposal (kg/12" equivalent wafer mask
layer) to 0.99 in 2022. Furthermore, TSMC has set a goal to reduce its outsourced unit waste
disposal per wafer to no more than 0.5 in 2030.
E GHG emissions 1
TSMC has continued its efforts to reduce greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions to drive low-carbon
manufacturing. In 2022, TSMC achieved net zero emissions of direct and indirect (from
purchased energy) GHG Emissions in overseas factories, and 100% use of
carbon-neutral natural gas in Taiwan fabs. It has set a target to achieve Net Zero Emissions by
2050.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 5

Server TAM estimate revisions

AI server racks: We raise our forecast for AI server racks powered by NVIDIA to 61k / 
97k / 149k in 2026 - 28E (vs. 50k / 92k / 148k previously), reflecting the increase in our 
forecasts for brand makers (Dell, SuperMicro, HP), leading to higher US CSP capex by 4% 
/ 17% / 22% in 2026-28E, and NVIDIA chipset shipments (raised by 9% / 15% in 2027 / 
28E), showing the ongoing upcycle of AI infrastructure. Overall, we expect the AI server 
rack global value TAM to grow at 163% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$1tn, or 63% of the 
global server value TAM by 2028E. By vendor, we continue to expect global leading CSPs 
to be the major customers, with leading ODM direct players as the major suppliers. We 
expect Hon Hai / FII to maintain the leading market position among ODMs and with 
continuous specification upgrade driving its global market share expansion.

AI servers: We raise AI server (8-GPU) equivalent shipment by 10% / 29% in 2027-28E 
to 2.7m / 3.3m in 2027-28E, mainly driven by rising ASIC-based AI server demand. The 
custom-design provides ASIC-based AI servers optimized for AI computation with better 
efficiency, such as lower latency, or memory savings via reduced expensive read/write 
cycles. We continue to see new generation ASICs launched by global leading CSPs, 
supporting AI server growth: we expect the AI servers (8-GPU) global value TAM to grow

### Page 6

at 38% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$318bn, or 20% of global server value TAM by 2028E. 
We reduce 2026E shipment by 7% to 1.7m, reflecting a shift toward higher density AI 
server racks driven by leading US CSPs.

General servers: We raise general server revenue by 15% / 13% / 11% in 2026-28E 
driven by high memory costs and ongoing specification upgrades driving AI workloads. 
Intel launched its Intel Xeon 6+ processors at Computex 2026 (link) to facilitate Agentic 
AI workloads, positioning: CPU servers as the control plane, driving system-level 
performance and efficiency under rising agentic AI workloads, while supporting data 
movement and sustained inferencing. Intel presented Xeon 7 Diamond Rapids in Aug 
2026 (link), with architectures for Agentic AI and Enterprise scale workloads, 
empowering efficient inference and edge computing. AMD has begun production ramp 
of its 6th Gen AMD EPYC CPUs (Venice) in May (link), which powers agentic AI and 
diverse workloads - running the agents, AI host nodes, and general purposes that turns 
intelligence into action with optimized TCO (total cost of ownership). We expect general 
servers’ global value TAM to grow at 22% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$271bn, or 17% of 
global server value TAM by 2028E.

CSP capex: Regarding customer spending, our US internet team forecasts that leading 
US cloud service providers (CSPs) in aggregate will grow their capex budgets by 84%/ 
52%/ 13% YoY in 2026-28E, with our estimates being raised by 4% / 17% / 22% in 
2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 2028E. Within the China internet space, we expect leading

### Page 7

Chinese cloud platforms to grow capex by 105%/ 21%/ 14% YoY in 2026-28E, with our 
estimates being raised by 14% / 15% / 11% in 2026-28E. The strong increase in CSP 
capex reflects the high memory costs and growing demand on AI workloads. In terms of 
capex structure, we estimate chipsets (GPU, ASIC, CPU) remain the major contributors, 
along with memory (HBM), and followed by networking and other components (e.g. 
cooling, power, chassis, rail kits, etc.). Compared to global CSPs, we estimate China CSP 
spending on chipsets would be more diversified across GPU and ASICs considering 
various local fabless suppliers and customers’ varying demand on AI scenarios / tasks. 
Read more: Microsoft, Amazon, Meta, Alphabet, Oracle, Baba, Tencent, Baidu.  
Key highlights on the recent Maia 300 announcement from MSFT: (1) Microsoft to 
expand its Azure Maia system to multi-gigawatt deployments, making Maia the primary 
inference system for Microsoft products and services, and (2) Microsoft plans to have 
multi-gigawatt Maia deployment beginning in 2027, with reports indicating a potential 
plan to deploy >1M Maia 300 chips in 2027 (Microsoft is covered by Gabriela Borges).

Improving 3Q GPM helped by better mix — Dynapack's preliminary 3Q26 results 
showed margin recovery yet weak PC sales remained the key drag on the earnings. 
3Q26 EPS of NT$2.33 (+72% q/q and +49% y/y) missed our/BBG est by 45%/27%, 
although we believe the earnings shortfall was well anticipated given the 3Q sales 
miss owing to the delayed shipment of one of the BBU products and weaker PC 
demand. GPM improved q/q on a richer product mix, with higher-margin BBU 
making up c.50% of 3Q sales on our estimate, while OPM recovered to 10.7%, 
+4.2ppt q/q and +1.3ppt y/y. Nevertheless, the sales shortfall constrained op 
leverage, leaving margin improvement insufficient to offset weaker sales. 
PSU vendor shortages weigh on Oct; 12kW ramp to drive Nov sales rebound — 
Our industry checks suggest that Dynapack's major PSU vendor has encountered 
component supply shortages (related to power management IC) and therefore 
delayed BBU pull-ins, weighing on shipments of Dynapack's existing 3kW and 5kW 
BBU products in Oct. We therefore expect Oct-26 BBU sales to decline slightly m/m. 
Meanwhile, PC sales should recover sequentially and more than to offset the BBU 
softness, supporting a slight m/m increase in group sales. Looking beyond Oct-26, 
we expect accelerating shipments of the new 12kW BBU series from Nov-26 to drive 
a strong rebound in BBU sales.

Implications — We reiterate our bullish stance on the BBU sector despite near-term
volatility from external supply-chain constraints, which is likely to put pressure on
near-term sentiment. We view the component-driven delays in product pull-ins
primarily as a shipment-timing issue, with underlying demand remaining strong into
2027E. We also see ample opportunity for Dynapack to gain market share from

Panasonic, its major Japanese peer, particularly as power architectures migrate
toward HVDC. This share-gain potential remains intact, reinforcing our confidence
in Dynapack's medium term BBU growth outlook.

Dynapack International (3211.TWO)

Dynapack International
Valuation
We assign a 32x target P/E to our 2027E EPS of NT$25 to derive a target price of NT$800. We value Dynapack based on 2027E
target P/E as we believe 2027E better reflects its earnings power after BBU capacity expansion. Our target P/E of 32x is based
on our forecast EPS CAGR of 66% in 2025-2027E and a PEG of 0.5x, which we benchmark against its major PSU partner, as we

expect Dynapack to grow alongside its partner.
Risks
Our quantitative model assigns a High Risk rating to Dynapack. However, we believe such a rating is not warranted for
Dynapack due to: 1) the company is generating solid revenue and earnings growth over next 3 years; the underlying industry,
BBU for AIDC, also enjoys secular growth; 2) the business model that involves with product customization and strict
safety/reliability requirements would imply relatively benign competition and therefore stable pricing and margins; and 3) the

company has a strong balance sheet and has been in net cash position since 2019.
Key downside risks to our price target/rating are: 1) Slower-than-expected BBU adoption in AIDC, 2) Delay in PSU customer
qualifications, 3) Competition from larger battery or power solution vendors, 4) Safety, certification and reliability issues.
Panasonic Holdings
(6752.T; ¥3957.0; 1; 16 Oct 26; 15:30)
Valuation
To derive our target price, we use a global sum-of-the-parts (SoTP) approach. In the case of Panasonic Holdings, we reference

52 other companies in the Citi coverage universe to set a fair value PER for each business division, which we sum to derive our
overall target price.
Panasonic fair-value PERs for individual business segments range from 8x to 31x, and have a weighted average of 26.2x
(excluding subsidies in the battery business), taking minority interest and equity in earnings into account. We estimate
business-derived equity value of ¥5,000/share. We calculate the present value of the amount we expect Panasonic to receive

in battery subsidies over five years at ¥400/share. Combining these two values, we derive a target price of ¥5,400.
Risks
Risks to our target price include 1) unsuccessful management policies or strategies resulting in weaker-than-expected
business investment returns or sales growth, or conversely producing greater-than-expected results at a relatively early stage,
2) stagnation in the global economy or an upturn in it, and rises or declines in demand in the electrical industry, and 3) forex

trends. If these factors manifest themselves differently than we have anticipated, the share price may vary from our target
price.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

## Document 192: 9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象

Source PDF: all report pdf/9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 7 日(水)

1/3 
9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象  
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・時事通信は10月６日付で「日銀が『基調的な物価上昇率』について、２％程度に到達したと
判断する方向で検討に入ることが５日、分かった」と報じた。記事内には10月29・30日開催
の金融政策決定会合で取りまとめる展望レポートで、そうした認識が記述されるとしてい
る。

・筆者はこの報道が現実になる可能性が高いと判断している。日銀は予てから基調的物価上昇
率が２％に到達する時期を「2026年度後半から2027年度」と予想してきたので（2026年度後
半にあたる）10月にそうした認識を示さないと、ハト派的な印象を与えてしまうことにな
る。

・既に日銀の政策態度は基調的物価上昇率を２％に「引き上げる」段階から、２％以上に「上
振れさせない」段階に移行している。局面が変化した理由としては、やはり原油高とそれに
対する企業の価格設定行動の積極化が大きいだろう。従来、原油高は企業収益を圧迫するこ
とで、賃上げを阻害し、個人消費の停滞に繋がってきた。そうした環境下で内需は拡大しな
いため、原油高は短期的にインフレ要因となっても、やや長い目でみるとデフレ的な色彩を
帯びていた。もっとも、2026年春先以降の原油高局面では、政府の手厚い支援もあり、企業
・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12 月に 1.5％とした後、27 年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/3 
は価格転嫁を積極的に進めた。企業物価指数は４月に前月比＋2.8％と即座に上昇し、前年比
伸び率は８月時点でも＋7.6％と高止まりしている。日銀短観（６月および９月調査）では１
年後の販売価格見通しが急伸した。こうした早期かつ大幅な価格転嫁の進展によって、企業
収益は傷みにくくなっている。このことは、名目賃金と名目消費が伸びやすい構図にあるこ
とを浮き彫りにしている。

・日銀が、基調的物価上昇率が２％に到達したとの認識を示すのであれば、それは利上げペー
スの加速を自ら正当化することになる。さすがに10月の利上げはビハインド・ザ・カーブに
陥ったことを認めたと市場参加者に受け止められる恐れがあるため、見送られると予想する
が、（前回利上げから約３ヶ月が経過する）12月会合では利上げが決定されるだろう。そう
なれば、３～４ヶ月に一度の利上げが標準ペースとなることが市場参加者に広く意識される
だろう。

・利上げを巡っては、経済指標に加えて「政府との距離感」を考慮する必要があり、予想は難
儀であった。しかしながら、ここ数週間で情勢は変化したように感じる。９月25日に片山さ
つき財務相が「官邸とも相談の上、私から初めて申し上げるが、先日の日米首脳会談では、
為替について、トランプ大統領から円安への懸念が示された」と発言したことを皮切りに、
同日には城内経済再生相が「アベノミクス的な意味でのリフレーション政策は、もうそうい
う局面は終わっている」とした。また10月５日に公表された９月30日の経済財政諮問会議の

なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
ビューに応じて「（政策金利を）段階的に調整していく方針には賛成している」とした。９
月18日会合における「主な意見」で内閣府の出席者（おそらく城内大臣）が利上げに消極的
と読める発言をしていたことと整合しないが、それは9月18日から25日の間に政府の態度が変
化した可能性が指摘できる。

・10月会合までに円安が進む、あるいは原油高等の要因によってＦｅｄが利上げに踏み切る事
態となれば、日銀も追随する可能性があるものの、そうした状況に至らなければ、10月は
「既往の利上げ効果を見極めたい」などとして利上げを見送るだろう。

・なお、これらに鑑みて、筆者は2027年７月の審議委員人事が中立ないしはタカ派的な人選に
なると予想する。高田委員と田村委員に考え方の近い人物に白羽の矢が立ち、リフレ派が送
り込まれることはないと考えている。利上げに積極的な２名の後任人事にリフレ派と目され
る人物が浮上した場合、金融市場では長期金利上昇や円安の反応が予想される。

## Document 287: CoreWeave信贷危机更像2023年SVB恐慌，而非2008年次贷危机？

Source PDF: all report pdf/CoreWeave信贷危机更像2023年SVB恐慌，而非2008年次贷危机？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

当前的关键不是 CoreWeave 会不会倒，⽽是算⼒定价能否⽀撑起这个周期的融资逻辑。这
个问题的答案，将决定这场恐慌的最终归宿。
摘要：
CoreWeave ⽆担保债券收益率飙升⾄约 15% 、 CDS 利差突破 1000 个基点，令市场对整个 AI 资本
⽀出周期的系统性⻛险产⽣强烈质疑。
然⽽，华尔街头部机构正集体发声：这不是 2007 年新世纪⾦融（ New Century Financial ）式的
系统性崩塌，⽽更像 2023 年硅⾕银⾏（ SVB ）引发的阶段性恐慌 —— ⼀只规模极⼩、结构极脆
弱的⼯具，正被市场当作万亿美元 AI 资本⽀出周期的晴⾬表。
巴克莱全球宏观研究主管 Ajay Rajadhyaksha 指出， CoreWeave 约 15 亿美元的⽆担保债券在总
债务约 350 亿美元中属于结构性次级地位，以此⼯具的价格信号否定整个 AI 周期，与 2023 年⽤⼀
只约 40 亿美元的区域银⾏ ETF 预判美国经济衰退如出⼀辙。⾼盛则认为市场选错了参照系 ——

⽆担保债券市场并不代表 CoreWeave 真实的新增融资成本。
历史先例： SVB 还是 07 年⾦融危机？
这场争论的核⼼，是历史类⽐的选择。
2007 年 4 ⽉，美国最⼤次级抵押贷款机构新世纪⾦融申请破产，其后整个信贷市场逐步崩塌，标
普 500 指数在随后两年下跌约 40% 。 2023 年 3 ⽉，硅⾕银⾏被监管当局关闭，市场随即形成叙
事：区域银⾏是中⼩企业信贷命脉，信贷枯竭将触发衰退。债券市场迅速定价当年降息 75 ⾄ 100
个基点， VIX 恐慌指数飙升程度甚⾄超过 2007 年。
结果，美联储 2023 年⼀次未降息，反⽽继续加息，美国经济全年增速远超预期，衰退从未到
来。区域银⾏ ETF KRE 收复全部失地，标普 500 在随后两年上涨约 40% 。
彭博专栏作家 John Authers 指出，两次危机在实时均貌似严峻，最终⾛向却南辕北辙。SVB 是
⼀次被过度外推的恐慌，⽽⾮系统性⻛险的前兆；新世纪⾦融的破产则揭⽰了⼀个贯穿整个⾦
融体系的结构性缺陷。当前 CoreWeave 的处境，从形态上更接近 SVB 。

Rajadhyaksha 总结了 SVB 恐慌的核⼼错误： " 错误不在于认识到硅⾕银⾏已经倒闭。它确实倒
了。错误在于从⼀个⼩型、波动剧烈、结构脆弱的⼯具，外推⾄整个美国经济。 "
巴克莱： 15 亿美元的 " 尾巴 " 在摇动整条 " 狗 "
Rajadhyaksha 将当前市场恐慌的逻辑结构与 SVB 时期直接类⽐。
2023 年那条 " 尾巴 " ，是⼀只资产管理规模约 40 亿美元的等权重区域银⾏ ETF——KRE ，许多成
分股交投清淡。就是这只⼩ ETF 的下跌，带动了数万亿美元的利率、投资级信⽤和股票仓位重新
定价，形成⾃我强化的恐慌循环。
2026 年的 " 尾巴 " ，则是 CoreWeave 约 15 亿美元的结构性次级⽆担保债券。
Rajadhyaksha 梳理了 CoreWeave 的三层债务结构：最主要的是有担保的延迟提款定期贷款
（ DDTL ），存放于独⽴特殊⽬的载体（ SPV ）中，每个 SPV 背后绑定具体的 GPU 硬件、客⼾合
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

同和数据中⼼租约 —— 当 Meta 签署多年期 " ⽤不⽤都要付钱 " 的合同并转让给 SPV ，贷款的实质
抵押品是 Meta 的付款承诺；其次是约 90 亿美元的可转换债券，票息介于 1.75% ⾄ 2.875% ；最底
层才是⽆担保票据，清算时前⾯有数百亿美元债权排队。
" 1 5 % 的 收 益 率 告 诉 你 这 张 纸 的 ⼀ 些 信 息 ， 它 不 能 告 诉 你 这 家 公 司 即 将 停 ⽌ 运
营， "Rajadhyaksha 写道，并打了⼀个⽐⽅： " 如果⼀个⼈有 3500 万美元的房贷加上⼀张⼩额信
⽤卡，信⽤卡利率⾼，说明的是信⽤卡的问题，不能说明这座城市所有房贷借款⼈都有⿇烦。 "
数字对⽐同样直观： 2023 年 3 ⽉，市场因⼀只 40 亿美元的 ETF 下跌定价 100 个基点降息； 2026
年，市场因约 15 亿美元的结构性次级债券交投于 80 美分，开始质疑⼀个万亿美元级别的资本⽀
出周期。
⾼盛：市场选错了融资成本的参照系
⾼盛分析师 Gabriela Borges 和 Maura Hager 的核⼼论点，同样落在债务结构的误读上。

⾼盛指出，CoreWeave 的主要融资⼯具是 DDTL—— 以特定客⼾合同、 GPU 基础设施及相关现
⾦流为抵押的资产⽀持项⽬融资。贷款⽅承保的是具体合同的现⾦流质量，⽽⾮ CoreWeave 的
整体信⽤状况，这⼀结构将融资⻛险从公司资产负债表转移⾄底层资产，并降低了再融资⻛
险。⽆担保债券市场对 CoreWeave 的新发⾏已关闭数⽉，以该市场的收益率衡量融资成本，等
同于参照⼀个已失效的价格信号。
基于⼆季度利息⽀出年化 25.6 亿美元除以约 300 亿美元总债务，⾼盛估算 CoreWeave 当前加权平
均债务成本约为 800 ⾄ 900 个基点。对于新增融资，⾼盛假设投资级客⼾（如 Meta ）对应 DDTL
定价约为 SOFR+300 个基点，⾼收益客⼾约为 SOFR+600 个基点，混合成本区间 750 ⾄ 1050 个
基点，保守基准取 1000 个基点，意味着新增融资成本较当前仅上升 100 ⾄ 200 个基点。
⾼盛还援引市场数据： DDTL 5.0 和 5.5 均以不低于原始发⾏折价的价格交易，表明资产⽀持融资

市场对 CoreWeave 的信⼼尚未崩塌。
值得注意的是，⾼盛在报告披露附录中说明，其正担任 CoreWeave ⼀项待定承销业务的主承销
商或联席承销商，持有 CoreWeave 逾 1% 股份，过去 12 个⽉已从 CoreWeave 获得投资银⾏业务
报酬，并预计在未来三个⽉继续寻求相关业务。
⾼盛⽜市逻辑的⽀点与脆弱性：每兆⽡收⼊需提升 30%
⾼盛⽜市论点的另⼀半，建⽴在 CoreWeave 计算收费能⼒的上升预期之上。
⾼盛估算， CoreWeave ⽬前混合货币化⽔平约为每兆⽡年度经常性收⼊（ ARR ） 1000 万美元。
⽀撑上⾏的依据包括：短期合同（ 3 ⾄ 6 个⽉）签约价已达每兆⽡ 4000 万美元； CoreWeave 今年
7 ⽉提价 25% ， 10 ⽉再度提价 10% ；⼆季度签署的合同贡献利润率⽐近期合同⾼出 5 ⾄ 10 个百分
点。⾼盛将每兆⽡ 1300 万美元作为新签⻓期合同的 " 合理起点假设 " 。
代⼊⽰例模型（ 5 年期、 200 兆⽡合同， 80% 债务融资，融资成本 10% ，残值为零），可得 15% 的

⽆杠杆内部收益率，与 11.8% 的加权平均资本成本之间形成约 3 个百分点的正利差。
然⽽⾼盛的敏感性分析同样揭⽰了这⼀逻辑的脆弱性：若以当前实际混合⽔平 1000 万美元 / 兆⽡
计算，⽆杠杆内部收益率将跌⾄ 6.3% ，在同等融资成本下利差变为负 6 个百分点。整个⽜市逻辑
的成⽴，依赖于 CoreWeave 每兆⽡收⼊在现有基础上提升约 30% 。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
⾼盛⾃⾝模型数据也揭⽰了这条路径的漫⻓： 2026 年和 2027 年⾃由现⾦流均为负 282 亿美元，
2028 年为负 158 亿美元，⻓期债务将从今年的约 440 亿美元攀升⾄ 2028 年的约 910 亿美元，预计
2030 年⾃由现⾦流才能转正。
供应链数据：需求现实是 AI 周期的真正 " 兜底 "
Rajadhyaksha 将视线拉向供应链最深处，以硬数据反驳系统性崩塌的叙事。
台积电近期报告营收超出⾃⾝指引上限 10 亿美元，连续第六个季度创利润纪录，⾼性能计算营
收占⽐升⾄ 68% ，公司上调四季度增⻓指引。 ASML 同期录得创纪录销售额，营业利润翻倍以
上， CEO Christophe Fouquet 表⽰ 2027 年和 2028 年的计划供应量将⽆法完全满⾜客⼾需求，部
分客⼾已开始尝试预订 2029 年和 2030 年的设备。
五⼤超⼤规模云⼚商（微软、 Meta 、 Alphabet 、亚⻢逊、甲⻣⽂） 2026 年 AI 基础设施合计⽀出

约 7250 亿⾄ 8000 亿美元， 2027 年估算超过 1 万亿美元。 CoreWeave 的 2026 年资本⽀出指引约为
350 亿⾄ 390 亿美元，约占上述总量的 4% ⾄ 5% 。
" 即 便 C o r e W e a v e 完 全 停 ⽌⽀ 出 ， 我 们 损 失 的 也 只 是整 个 A I 资 本⽀ 出 的 4 % ⾄
5% ， "Rajadhyaksha 表⽰， " 超⼤规模云⼚商的⽀出主要依靠⾃由现⾦流，⽽⾮⽆担保债券市
场。从⼀家新兴云⼚商外推⾄全球最赚钱的公司之⼀，这个逻辑链条拉得太⻓了。 "
巴克莱也承认，这次与 2023 年存在⼀个关键差异：没有等价的央⾏⼯具可以充当安全⽹。2023
年，美联储推出银⾏定期融资计划（ BTFP ），允许银⾏以⾯值质押⽔下债券换取流动性，成为
打断恐慌循环的关键。 AI 基础设施融资没有类似的政策⼯具。但 Rajadhyaksha 认为，超⼤规模
云⼚商对算⼒的真实需求本⾝，构成了这⼀周期⾃⼰的 " 兜底逻辑 " 。
Rajadhyaksha 坦⾔： " 市场有⼀种倾向，总是在复杂系统中找到最⼩、最波动的⼯具，然后把它

当作整体的晴⾬表。 2023 年是 KRE ， 2026 年是 CoreWeave 的⽆担保债券。两次信号都是真实
的。 2023 年的外推是错的，我们认为 2026 年的外推同样是错的。 "
真正的⻛险触发点：算⼒租⾦能否持守
尽管华尔街头部机构集体 " 灭⽕ " ，但各⽅均明确了⼀个共同的⻛险触发条件。
Rajadhyaksha 指出，AI 资本⽀出周期是否重蹈次贷覆辙，关键在于算⼒租⾦能否维持在⾜以覆
盖资本成本的⽔平。 " 若租⾦未能如预期那般⾼，可能引发真正的危机。 CoreWeave 是⼀个令⼈
警觉的情况，但它并不能提供这⽅⾯的证据。 "
⾼盛的敏感性分析同样指向这⼀核⼼脆弱性：若每兆⽡ ARR ⽆法从当前 1000 万美元升⾄ 1300 万
美元，整个正利差逻辑将全⾯逆转。⽽⼀旦供给追上需求、定价回落， CoreWeave 仍被锁定在
⻓达 7 ⾄ 16 年的数据中⼼租约中，退出代价⾼昂。
Equity Armor Investments 的 Brian Stutland 认为， CoreWeave 的问题核⼼在于其⾃⾝的商业模

式，⽽⾮ AI 的整体前景，但他也坦承这是⼀个 " 硬币的两⾯ "—— 究竟是孤⽴的公司层⾯问题，还
是揭⽰了更⼴泛的结构性脆弱性，⽬前仍是 " 各占⼀半的概率 " 。
对投资者⽽⾔，当前的关键不是 CoreWeave 会不会倒，⽽是算⼒定价能否⽀撑起这个周期的融
资逻辑。这个问题的答案，将决定这场恐慌的最终归宿。

### Page 4

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

## Document 314: Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm một lần tăng nữa trong năm (2026-09-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm một lần tăng nữa trong năm (2026-09-17).pdf
Report source: KBSV (Thematic)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm 
một lần tăng nữa trong năm 
Đánh giá áp lực lên tỷ giá và lãi suất Việt Nam

Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

### Page 2

— Chúng tôi dự báo vĩ mô trong nước đối với: (1) tỷ giá USD/VND tới cuối năm 2026 đi 
ngang so với cuối 2025 nhờ nguồn cung USD tích cực (từ kiều hối, FDI giải ngân và nâng 
hạng thị trường vốn) và chênh lệch lãi suất VND-USD dù có xu hướng thu hẹp nhưng 
vẫn duy trì trạng thái dương; và (2) lãi suất huy động sẽ duy trì ở vùng hiện tại trong 
phần còn lại của năm và kỳ vọng có thể giảm nhẹ nếu lạm phát được kiểm soát và 
NHNN sẽ có biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh khoản thị trường tích cực hơn.

debt-to-GDP was basically in line with Germany’s and well below Italy’s, but today it’s far
above Germany’s and closing in on Italy’s. Outside of the temporary Covid spike, Italian
debt/GDP has been stable for over a decade.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand  has even widened this year . Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months . Retail sales have barely 
increased, the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting 
economic growth are expected to increase in the second half of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.0% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 2.8% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.4% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would ease slightly to 1.2% in 2026 (1.3% in 2025), before edging back to 1.3% in 2027. The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

The Week Ahead October 9, 2026

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Monetary Authority of Singapore (MAS)

We expect another slight increase in the 
SGD NEER policy band slope at the MAS’ 
October review, with no change to the 
band’s width or centre. Import cost 
pressures and resilient growth favour a 
calibrated tightening, rather than a more 
forceful adjustment, in line with the 
global monetary policy stance.

Also due to be released alongside the 
MAS’ policy decision are the 3Q26 
advance GDP estimates, which we 
expect to show another quarter of strong 
growth at 5.4% yoy (1.4% qoq sa), 
compared with 5.9% yoy (1.4% qoq sa) in 
2Q26. Robust economic performance 
continued to be driven by artificial 
intelligence ( AI)-led trade activity, 
supporting the manufacturing and 
wholesale trade sectors, while strength 
in the financial sector underpinned the 
resilience of the modern services cluster. 
This came despite a moderation of the 
transport & storage sector, and 
weakness in consumer -facing food 
services and retail sectors.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect Malaysia’s advance GDP 
estimate for 3Q26 to register above -
trend growth of 5.0% yoy, albeit 
moderating from 6.0% yoy  in 2Q26. The 
overall expansion continued to be 
supported by ongoing strength in the 
export-oriented manufacturing sector, 
underpinned by strong  AI-related 
demand for electronics, and sustained 
domestic demand, backed by healthy 
investment and private consumption 
trends. However, contraction in mining 
and weak agricultural activity dragged 
overall growth.

Singapore

We expect firm Singapore non -oil 
domestic exports (NODX) growth of 28.0% 
yoy in September 2026, although 
moderating from August’s exceptional 
surge of 46.2%. Although favourable 
base effects largely faded in September, 
electronics shipments likely remained 
the primary growth driver, underpinned 
by robust AI -related demand amid 
sustained global investment in AI 
infrastructure, while non-electronics 
exports were uneven.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 9, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

China

Exports growth is expected to remain 
robust at 25.7% y oy in September, 
supported by continued strength in 
electronics demand. Both the official and 
Caixin manufacturing PMIs rose above 
the 50 expansion threshold during the 
month, pointing to improving 
manufacturing activity. External trade 
indicators also stren gthened, with 
average daily deadweight tonnage 
handled at China's 20 major ports 
accelerating from -0.7% yoy in August to 
7.2% in September. Freight activity 
improved as well, as growth in 
international cargo flights picked up 
from -1.4% y oy to 3.6% over the same 
period.

On the price front, factory -gate and raw 
material prices rebounded amid the 
renewed escalation in geopolitical 
tensions. Main raw material purchasing 
price and producer price subPMI 
increased from 56.6 and 50.4 to 60.8 and 
54.0 during the period. However, 
downstream consumer price pressures 
are likely to remain relatively subdued 
under the pricing-band mechanism. As a 
result, CPI inflation is expected to edge 
up from 0.8% y oy in August to 1.0% in 
September. Nevertheless, soft domestic 
demand is expected to continue 
constraining the pace of consumer price 
increases.

India

September inflation is likely to gather 
momentum to 5.7% y/y, partly driven by 
base effects, from 4.8% the month 
before. Food segments continue to 
register gains, with pressures 
broadening to include perishables, 
edible oils, rice, pulses, sugar, etc., 
according to high-frequency data. Under 
non-food, retail pump prices have not 
been adjusted further, though other fuel 
products, such as non -subsidised LPG, 
diesel and ATF (still up by double digits 
y/y, though slower than in August), are 
up on the year. Core  inflation is also 
likely to tick up, aligning with the central 
bank's view that price risks are no longer 
benign. Pressures have built up due to 
the overhang of an uneven and sub -par 
monsoon, drought conditions in parts of 
the country, and elevated oil pr ices. In 
the face of higher headline prints, we 
expect the RBI to keep the door open for 
another rate hike in December 2026.

## Document 430: Westpac Nowcast Q3 2026_ Second Estimate (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Westpac Nowcast Q3 2026_ Second Estimate (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 7

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
WESTPAC NOWCAST Q3
2026: SECOND ESTIMATE
Growth falters through the quarter
16 October 2026
Key points
• The second estimate from Westpac-Now
points to GDP growth of around +0.5%qtr
in Q3 2026 and 2.1%yr. This is slightly lower
than the first estimate of +0.6%qtr. This

still represents a slight step up in growth
from Q2 2026 but masks some loss of
momentum over the course of the quarter.
• Our Monthly Activity Index deteriorated
noticeably in August and September.
Weakness has become increasingly
broad-based, with labour and business
condition indicators among the
largest drags and relatively few areas
providing a meaningful offset.
• The economy is coming under increasing
pressure as the cumulative impact of
higher interest rates combines with

renewed disruptions in global energy
markets and a deepening housing
downturn. With another rate hike likely in
November, these pressures are expected
to build further heading into 2027.
• Overall, the economy is becoming
increasingly reliant on the upswing in
business investment to support growth.
This narrow base of growth has left the
balance of risks less favourable. Our central
case remains one of slowing rather than
stalling. Westpac-Now’s current model

dynamics point to growth moderating
to around +0.4%qtr in Q4 2026. Our
formal forecasts have growth slowing
to a softer +0.2%qtr pace (see here).
• Our final Westpac-Now estimate for Q3
2026 will be published on 20 November.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
In November 2025, we introduced Westpac-Now (see here),
a continuously-updated measure of current activity in the
Australian economy. Westpac-Now draws on more than
60 high frequency economic and financial variables, using
advanced statistical techniques to provide a timely pulse

check on economic momentum. It is the first Australian
model to incorporate internal banking data, enhancing its
predictive power.
This note provides the second estimate of GDP growth for the
September quarter 2026, using domestic and international
data released up to and including the September Labour Force
Survey (released 15 October 2026). Updates are published
monthly (see Appendix A for the release schedule).
Westpac Monthly Activity Index: activity falters as

headwinds mount
After showing tentative signs of improvement through the
second half of 2025, the Westpac Monthly Activity Index has
weakened markedly over 2026. The Index moved back into
negative territory in February and has declined further since.
While there were some signs of stabilisation around mid-year,
this proved to be short lived with the Index falling to –0.88 in
September, outside of the pandemic period, the weakest read
since 2013.
The economy is coming under pressure from multiple

fronts, with key headwinds coming from higher interest
rates, renewed disruptions to global energy markets and a
deepening in the housing downturn. Among households,
spending held up surprisingly well through the first half of the
year despite historically weak sentiment, but more recently
Growth momentum on shaky ground
spending outside of fuel has started to cool (see here). At
the same time, the labour market has continued to soften,
with unemployment reaching a cycle high of 4.7% (see here).

Meanwhile, the business sector is also showing signs of
softening, with sentiment deteriorating and conditions holding
at weak levels (see here). Business credit growth remains
a notable exception, although even here there are tentative
signs of easing (see here).
This deterioration is particularly evident in our heatmap.
Negative contributions have become both broader and more
pronounced through 2026, with the weakness intensifying
in August and September. Labour and business condition

indicators are now the largest sources of weakness, while
spending and confidence have also deteriorated materially.
Credit and capacity utilisation indicators have been
comparatively less negative, but there are currently few areas
providing a meaningful offset to the broader softening.
Our indicator heatmap decomposes changes in the Monthly
Activity Index into group-level contributions, allowing us to
assess which components are driving movements in activity.

For ease of interpretation, indicators are grouped into broad
categories (see the Appendix for further details). For example,
the labour market category includes both official ABS Labour
Force Survey outcomes and timelier survey-based measures of
labour demand and job advertisements.
Westpac-Now: Resilient growth outlook slowing not
stalling
Recall that Westpac-Now is a refined indicator that combines
our Monthly Activity Index with advanced econometric

techniques to estimate GDP growth. As previously noted,
the fit is not expected to be exact. Measured GDP includes
Neha Sharma
Economist

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
components that are not well captured by high-frequency
activity indicators, such as imputed rents for owner-occupiers,
housing-related ownership transfer costs, public and business
inventories, and depreciation.
To bridge monthly and quarterly data, we use a MIDAS

framework, allowing us to combine mixed-frequency inputs
and incorporate partial information for the current quarter
when producing GDP forecasts.
Based on current model estimates, Westpac-Now’s second
estimate for Q3 2026 points to GDP growth of 0.5%qtr
(with a 70% confidence interval of –0.1%qtr to 1.0%qtr)
and 2.1%yr, representing a slight downgrade from the first
estimate of +0.6%qtr but still a step up from the 0.4%qtr
(2.1%yr) outcome recorded in Q2 2026. While aggregate

growth appears to have held up through Q3, the underlying
picture became softer as the quarter progressed. At the
second decimal place the estimate is 0.46%qtr, leaving it
close to rounding down to 0.4%qtr.
With several headwinds now bearing on the economy
simultaneously, there is a growing risk that the slowdown in
activity becomes more pronounced than currently anticipated.
At the same time, the strength of the business investment
upswing provides an important counterweight that should

help cushion the broader slowdown.
Looking ahead, model dynamics suggest growth will tick
down to 0.4%qtr in Q4 2026 before picking up in Q1 2027.
However, these projections remain much more tentative,
given their reliance on projected inputs rather than realised
data. In addition, heightened uncertainty, particularly around
the renewed tensions in the Middle East conflict, means risks
around these forward estimates are larger than usual.
September Quarter data: a softer end to the quarter

Data released in the first month of the September quarter
indicated that the economy has picked up momentum relative
to the previous quarter, with our first nowcast estimating Q3
growth at 0.6%qtr, in line with the 0.6%qtr “pure forecast”
based solely on data available up to June 2026.
However, the flow of data since then shows downside risks to
growth have begun to materialise. Incorporating the August,
followed by the September (to date), data both yielded

nowcasts of 0.5%qtr (with a slight softening at the second
decimal place). This contrasts with Q2 2026, when weakness
emerged early in the quarter, in April, before subsequent data
pointed to stabilisation.
Note that monthly activity estimates are more uncertain
than normal when inflation is shifting. Faster price growth
can cause nominal indicators, such as consumer spending,
to rise more strongly even though ‘real’, inflation-adjusted
spending may be softening (and vice versa). The inclusion

of ‘real’ indicators, such as employment and banking
transaction volumes, helps mitigate some – but not all – of this
uncertainty.
Bottom line:
Westpac-Now points to GDP growth of around 0.5%qtr
(2.1%yr) in Q3 2026, suggesting aggregate growth held up
reasonably well through the quarter, although the flow of
data points to softer momentum as the quarter progressed.
The substantial upswing in business investment remains
an important source of support, helping to offset mounting

weakness elsewhere in the economy.
Looking ahead, the accumulation of headwinds is expected to
see growth slow further towards year-end, with Westpac-Now
currently pointing to growth of around 0.4%qtr in Q4 2026.
Downside risks around the outlook have increased, but for now
our central case remains one of slower growth rather than a
material downturn.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Appendix A: Westpac-Now Release Schedule (2026)
Going forward, Westpac Now will be published on the Friday following each ABS Labour Force Survey (LFS) release, at 11:30am AEDT/AEST (Canberra time).
Each release incorporates the latest labour market data alongside other high frequency indicators and provides an updated nowcast for quarterly GDP growth.

As the quarter progresses, each update should be interpreted as a progressively more complete estimate of that quarter’s economic performance.
Release date (Friday) LFS reference month Quarter focus Interpretation
20 November 2026 October 2026 Q3 2026 – final estimate Near complete read on Q3 activity
18 December 2026 November 2026 Q4 2026 – first estimate Initial assessment of Q4 momentum

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
NOVEMBER CPI
PREVIEW AND ANALYSIS
Complete Montly CPI will take some time to understand
19 December 2025
Key points
• The October monthly CPI was 
flat in the month, broadly as we 
expected but stronger than market 
expectations. For more information see 
“Electrical noise is far from static”.
• The impact on our inflation profile 
was marginal. The December quarter 
CPI remains 0.6%qtr/3.6%yr, while 
the Trimmed Mean edged up from 
0.7%qtr/3.1%yr to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr.
• Our November monthly CPI estimate 
is 0.4% for the month, leaving the 
annual pace unchanged at 3.8%yr.
• The introduction of the complete 
monthly CPI is a significant enhancement 
to our analysis of price formation in 
the Australian economy. However, 
with many series having only a short 
history, it will take time to fully 
understand some of the finer details.
• For now, we will continue to place 
greater emphasis on the quarterly 
measures of core inflation.

### Page 2

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
No surprise in the October CPI
The new complete monthly CPI gained 3.8% in the year to 
October, coming in a touch softer than Westpac’s estimate of 
3.9%yr but a bit stronger than the market estimate of 3.6%yr. 
So while the market and other commentators may have been 
surprised by the result it was broadly within our expectations 
and as such has had just a marginal impact on our inflation 
profile.
The October CPI was flat in the month, a touch softer than 
Westpac’s published near-cast of 0.1% on the back of a larger 
than expected decline in electricity (–10.2%) which was only 
partially offset by a smaller than expected decline in auto fuel 
(–0.9%) and an unexpected bump up in garments (+2.6%).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) lifted 0.3% in the month of October, 
the same monthly increase it has seen for the previous two 
months and down from the 0.5%mth increase in July but 
stronger than the 0.2%mth prints from March to June.
This saw the annual pace of the TM increase to 3.3% from 
3.2% in September. This month the TM excluded the annual 
rise in electricity; alongside other large price rises and falls. 
Due to the short history of many series in the monthly CPI, the 
annual pace of monthly TM inflation can only be calculated 
back to April 2025. Before then the ABS notes that the annual 
pace is calculated from the quarterly CPI data.
For more information on the October monthly CPI see 
“Electrical noise is far from static ”.
Monthly CPI points to some momentum
Following the October CPI we have seen some minor revisions 
to the various components in our December quarter CPI 
estimate with the upward revisions, such as transport via auto 
fuel and motor vehicles, being offset by downward revisions 
to recreation, alcohol & tobacco and utilities in particular. This 
has left our December CPI estimate unchanged at 0.6%qtr.   
As our Chief Economist, Luci Ellis, has noted that inflation 
did see a bump in the September quarter and again in the 
month of October. However, the main sources of the surprise 
have had little to do with domestic demand or labour market 
pressures. Rather, a sizeable part of the bump looks to have 
been from administered prices and noise. Since the release of 
the data, the RBA has recognised this issue but at the post 
RBA Board Decision Media Conference in December, the 
Governor communicated that the Bank is more worried about 
upside risks to inflation than first thought.
We agree that some of this momentum will continue into the 
December quarter as highlighted by our upward revision in our 
We will continue to focus on the quarterly prints
Justin Smirk

BULLETIN
December quarter TM estimate to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr from our 
earlier estimate of 0.7%qtr/3.1%yr. 
For the December quarter, price changes that are included in 
the TM go from a low of –0.26% to a high of 1.66%. Readers 
will remember that the TM is calculated from seasonally 
adjusted price changes and not the original estimates so if 
unaffected by regular seasonal price shifts. We estimate 
that the most significant components to be trimmed off 
the top in the quarter are: electricity (2.7%); other services 
for motor vehicles (2.5%); tobacco (2.1%); and automotive 
fuel (1.7%). Trimmed off the bottom are: other non-durable 
household products (–0.39%); garments for women (–0.92%); 
international holiday travel (–0.97%); vegetables (–1.16%) and 
wine (–1.27%).   
However, we do not expect this momentum to continue 
through 2026. As Luci noted in her article “ RBA on hold 
in 2026, risks on both sides ” we expect inflation to keep 
easing, due to the lagged impact of past restrictive policy 
and the unwind of earlier government support measures. We 
believe that many commentators have overlooked these lags. 
Monetary policy typically takes a year or more to fully affect 
jobs and prices, so current outcomes reflect the peak cash rate 
from a year ago and we expect to see further labour market 
softening. Strong hiring across the care economy has masked 
some of that restrictiveness, as has public infrastructure 
spending, but these supports are now fading along with last 
year’s tax-cut boost. We believe that even with the 75bps in  
rate cuts this year, policy remains mildly restrictive, and given 
usual lags, this will keep showing in the data through 2026.
Taking a look at some of the recent larger moves in monthly 
prices can highlight many of the issues Luci raises. Taking the 
seasonally adjusted data, smoothing it with a three month 
average then calculating the change over three months (so we 
have three months on three months percentage change) we 
find at the top end of the list some price increases you would 
not expect to continue at the same pace. 
For example at the top are: lamb & goat (8.1%, highly 
seasonal); accessories (8.1% with jewellery prices surging 
on the back of record high gold prices); audio visual & 
computing services (6.0% due to Netflix subscriptions); beef 
& veal (3.7%, highly seasonal); coffee & tea (3.6% noting 
global coffee prices have passed their peak and are already 
significantly lower); water & sewerage (2.3% IPART decision 
was for a 13.8% increase for Sydney Water in October); 
property rates & charges (2.3% administered prices); and 
electricity (1.2%, reflecting unwinding of cost of living 
rebates). Offsetting some of these increases was falling prices 
for: men’s footwear (–2.0%); vegetables (–2.0% seasonal); 
eggs (–1.5%, reflecting the unwinding of the price spike 
associated with an outbreak of bird flu); and major household 
appliances (–1.2%). 
Core inflation still to moderate through 2026
We are projecting headline inflation to get back to the RBA’s 
target band, but not until later in 2026. We now see core 
inflation back below the midpoint of the 2–3% range at the 
end of the year. We had thought this might be achieved by the 
third quarter but now expect current momentum to continue

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
for a bit longer. However, it is worth noting that our estimate
of the six month annualised pace of the TM is down to 2.4%yr
by June 2026.
Electricity to boost November CPI
Westpac is estimating a 0.4% increase in the November

monthly CPI which will hold the annual pace flat at 3.8%yr.
The seasonally adjusted estimate is 0.5% in the month
suggesting it is a stronger than usual estimate.
Boosting the November estimate is: a 16% surge in electricity
(as the impact of the last round of rebates fades); a 3.2% jump
in gas & other household fuels; and a 2.3% bump in auto fuel
(based on reported pump prices). Partially offsetting these
gains is a –2.6% decline in holiday travel & accommodation

and a –2.9% drop in clothing & footwear.
Take care with the Monthly CPI
Many series in the monthly CPI only date back to early 2024
so seasonal adjustment is somewhat problematic. Some
series did have a longer monthly history coming from the
previously published monthly CPI indicator and it appears
the ABS may have used some of this to seasonally adjust the
data. However, we caution that some of the new data has a
different history to the old data and as such, we expect it is

going to take some time to understand the seasonal behaviour
of the new data.
For example, for male garments there is some difference
with the reported monthly change compared to the old CPI
Indicator series but this appears to be only marginal. However,
for women’s, the difference can be significant with even a
reversal of the sign of change in some recent months. And for
some series with a short history and no monthly indicator data
to compare to some of the adjustment appears to be a little

arbitrary, as you can see in the chart right which compares the
children’s garments index in original and seasonally adjusted
terms. The only adjustment made to the series was in June
2025 when a –3.2% change was adjusted to a flat print. Why?
We are yet to uncover.
You can also see where the ABS has turned to smoothing
processes to seasonally adjust short data series. In the chart
over the seasonally adjusted series looks as if has been
generated by applying a Henderson Trend to the original

children’s footwear series.
As such, we expect it is possible there will be significant
adjustments made to the seasonally adjustment process as
data is collected which could result in significant revisions to
seasonally adjusted series including the Trimmed Mean and
the Weighted Median.

## Document 441: “铝大庄”回来了，这次押注铜！

Source PDF: all report pdf/“铝大庄”回来了，这次押注铜！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

资深⾦属交易员 Sonny McNess 在 Hartree Partners ⼤举买⼊ LME ⼗⽉铜合约、做空⼗⼀
⽉，赌特朗普对精炼铜加征关税将触发近⽉溢价飙升。⽬前该头⼨已是市场最⼤，⼗⽉合
约⼀度较⼗⼀⽉溢价 91.99 美元。此前 McNess 在铝市的⼤仓位操作曾直接逼使 LME 修改规
则。
摘要：
曾因在铝市场⼤举押注⽽震动伦敦⾦属交易所（ LME ）的资深⾦属交易员 Sonny McNess ，如今
携重仓回归 —— 这⼀次，他的⽬标是铜。
据彭博社 10 ⽉ 21 ⽇报道，知情⼈⼠透露， McNess ⽬前担任 Hartree Partners LP ⾸席⾦属衍⽣品
交易员，近⼏周⼤量买⼊ LME ⼗⽉交割铜合约，同时做空⼗⼀⽉期货，押注短期供应紧张将推
动近⽉合约溢价⾛⾼。知情⼈⼠表⽰， Hartree 的头⼨是近期多个⼤型⼗⽉交割合约持仓中规模
最⼤的⼀个。此番操作的核⼼逻辑在于：⼀旦特朗普宣布对精炼铜加征关税，短期供应挤压⼏
乎不可避免，相关头⼨将直接获利。
这⼀押注已在市场上掀起波澜。⼗⽉合约到期前，⼗⽉与⼗⼀⽉价差剧烈波动，⼗⽉合约⼀度

较⼗⼀⽉溢价⾼达每吨 91.99 美元，呈现出供应紧张时期常⻅的现货升⽔（ backwardation ）结
构。与此同时，这场交易正值⾏业年度盛会 LME Week 在伦敦举⾏，⼤仓位与剧烈波动已成为与
会者热议的焦点话题。
关税预期驱动铜市博弈
特朗普是否对铜加征关税，是当前铜市最核⼼的悬念。
据彭博报道，⾃今年 5 ⽉以来， LME 全球仓储⽹络中的铜库存⼤幅下滑 —— 交易商提前将⼤量铜
转移⾄美国，以规避潜在进⼝关税⻛险。与此同时，中国进⼝需求回升也进⼀步收紧了铜市供
应。
库存持续缩减推动价格频繁进⼊现货升⽔状态。今年 8 ⽉，现货铜价⼀度较三个⽉期货溢价⾼达
每吨 545 美元，创下 LME 历史上最⼤规模供应挤压⾏情之⼀。
⽬前， LME ⼤量⾦属库存集中于其美国仓库。若关税落地，交易商可通过买⼊近⽉ LME 合约、
从交易所提取⾦属并交割⾄纽约期货合约的⽅式套利获利。然⽽，特朗普政府迟迟未有明确表
态，部分交易商开始担忧：若关税最终未能推进，铜价可能⾯临⼤幅回调⻛险。
McNess 其⼈： LME 的 “ ⼤仓位惯犯 ”

Sonny McNess 在⾦属交易圈颇具名⽓，以敢于下重注著称，其历次⼤规模押注多次震动 LME ，
甚⾄迫使交易所修改规则。
他此前曾在摩根⼤通和 Mercuria Energy Group 任职，主要专注于铝市场。今年早些时候，他
离开 Mercuria ，随后加⼊ Hartree Partners ，出任⾸席⾦属衍⽣品交易员。
去年， McNess 在 Mercuria 任职期间于铝市场建⽴⼤规模头⼨，直接促使 LME 推出所谓 " 近⽉借
贷规则 " （ front-month lending rules ） ——该规则强制要求持仓量超过 LME 仓库现货库存的交易
商，须以零成本将超额头⼨借出，以防⽌⼤仓位对价格产⽣不当影响。
